# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
  document. §8 below turns its fourth row into a fixed vocabulary.
- **Composed reads must not fabricate.** Unknown totals stay unknown; revoked
  rows do not linger. Both flips below preserve this; they change what is
  *known*, not what is *claimed*.
- **Writes have one lifecycle with durable admission distinct from commit
  and stable operation identity.** Keep LLP 1018.004's three outcomes.
- **Rust and SQLite.** Yes, and the engine's transaction, interpreter, rule
  evaluation and successor logic are the pieces to copy first (§5).
- **Prototype the experience before the core.** Yes — and §8 makes the
  prototype's API signatures binding on the implementation, not the reverse.
- **Deterministic simulation early**, citing TigerBeetle. This is the single
  most important sentence in LLP 2000 and §11 makes it the primary harness.
- **A real deployment and a native client early.** Yes; §11 puts the native
  client in slice one.
- **Parallel, fenced, 2000-series, promotion is the author's later call.** Yes.

## 4. Where LLP 2000 is wrong or leaves the wrong things open

### 4.1 It reopens the language and leaves the bound model open; invert that

§6 asks to "evaluate Quarry" and "evaluate any alternative against the same
contracts." The one-language decision took thirty-seven paper applications,
a three-family panel, a spelling spike and an author ruling (LLP
0329.014.004 §9, LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b) to close, and the shipped result is
the most readable artifact in Snapback 2: the chat schema is 129 lines and a
query is three to six. Reopening the language will consume the first month.
What *should* be reopened is the bound model under it, which §6 phrases as a
comparison to "concentrate early investigation on." Half of the Quarry
refusals a builder meets are the certifier's byte and step prices wearing
Quarry syntax; the `fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap (LLP 1018.008
F4) are bounded-composition restrictions the pricing model forced. Decide
the bound model (§6), keep the language, and re-derive its restrictions.

### 4.2 The first slice is at the scale where Snapback 2 looked elegant

§10 proposes "a private conversation with paginated messages and an unread
count," "two principals and two clients." Snapback 2 passed that slice in
its first week. It broke at the hub: 1,000 memberships refused the digest
fold, made global search cost a thousand requests, and refused the
envelopes builders first wrote (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). Every one of those
was discovered by a cold builder on a published package weeks after the
architecture was fixed. The first slice must include the hub principal from
day one (§11).

### 4.3 It investigates the client data model; the field has answered

exec
/bin/bash -lc "timeout 10s rg -n 'live|presence|connected|totals|transaction|privacy|ceiling' snapback4/NOT-DOING.md" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w15-rules5
 succeeded in 0ms:
17:- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
21:- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
22:- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
26:- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
30:- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
38:- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.
59:- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
61:- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).
65:- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
98:  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.
127:  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
133:- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).
137:- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.
139:- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
141:- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
148:- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

/bin/bash -lc 'timeout 10s cat snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/presence.rs snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/residency/presence.rs' in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w15-rules5

//! Grace belongs to effect admission, never to predicted rows.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::RuntimeError, ir::{Expr, Stmt}, value::Value};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};

pub(super) type Dependencies = BTreeMap<String, Option<i64>>;
pub(crate) type Emission = (String, String, Value, Dependencies);

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub(super) enum Scalar { Bool(bool), Count(i64) }
#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
impl Scalar {
    pub fn value(self) -> Value { match self { Self::Bool(b) => Value::Bool(b), Self::Count(n) => Value::Int(n) } }
    pub fn of(value: &Value) -> Option<Self> { match value { Value::Bool(b) => Some(Self::Bool(*b)), Value::Int(n) => Some(Self::Count(*n)), _ => None } }
}

#[derive(Clone)]
pub(super) struct Tracked {
    pub deps: Dependencies,
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    pub absent: Option<Scalar>,
}
impl std::ops::Deref for Tracked { type Target = Dependencies; fn deref(&self) -> &Dependencies { &self.deps } }

#[derive(Default)]
struct Guard {
    yes: bool, no: bool,
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    setup: bool,
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    is_setup: bool,
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    emit_yes: bool,
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    emit_no: bool,
}

#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct EffectPresence {
    pub enabled: bool,
    pub reads: Dependencies,
    pub guard: Dependencies,
    pub variables: BTreeMap<String, Tracked>,
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    pub absent: Option<Scalar>,
    guards: BTreeMap<String, Guard>,
}

pub(super) fn bytes(deps: &Dependencies) -> u64 { deps.keys().map(|key| key.len() as u64 + 64).sum() }

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn prepare_effect_presence(&mut self, program: &crate::ir::Program) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.effect_presence.enabled = self.schema.operations.get(&program.name).is_some_and(|fact| fact.connected_effects_only);
        self.meter.at(&program.name);
        if self.effect_presence.enabled {
            #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
            let mut setups = Setups::default();
            plan_guards(&program.body, &program.name, &mut BTreeMap::new(), &mut self.effect_presence.guards, &mut self.meter, #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] &mut setups)?;
            #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
            if !self.device {
                needed_setups(&program.body, &mut Bindings::default(), &self.effect_presence.guards, &mut setups);
                mark_bindings(&setups, &mut self.effect_presence.guards);
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — reserve every potential dependency on every host, including absent/live principals; grace cannot decide a mutation refusal.
    pub(super) fn grace_read(&mut self, principal: &str, deadline: Option<i64>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.meter.heap(principal.len() as u64 + 64)?;
        self.effect_presence.reads.insert(principal.into(), deadline);
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(super) fn read_presence_value(&mut self, expr: &Expr) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if !self.effect_presence.enabled { return Ok(()); }
        // Include borrowed field/scalar fast paths, which do not call expr
        // on their root variable. Values themselves stay untouched.
        let name = match expr {
            Expr::Variable(name) => Some(name),
            Expr::Field { base, .. } => return self.read_presence_value(base),
            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => {
                for arg in args { self.read_presence_value(arg)?; }
                None
            }
            _ => None,
        };
        if let Some(deps) = name.and_then(|name| self.effect_presence.variables.get(name)) {
            self.meter.heap(bytes(deps))?;
            self.effect_presence.reads.extend(deps.iter().map(|(principal, deadline)| (principal.clone(), *deadline)));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn value_presence(&mut self) -> Result<Dependencies, RuntimeError> {
        self.meter.heap(bytes(&self.effect_presence.reads) + bytes(&self.effect_presence.guard))?;
        let mut deps = self.effect_presence.guard.clone();
        deps.extend(self.effect_presence.reads.iter().map(|(principal, deadline)| (principal.clone(), *deadline)));
        Ok(deps)
    }

    pub(super) fn bind_effect_value(&mut self, name: &str, value: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        self.bind_delivery(name);
        let deps = self.value_presence()?;
        self.env.insert(name.into(), value);
        if deps.is_empty() { self.effect_presence.variables.remove(name); }
        else {
            self.meter.heap(name.len() as u64 + 64)?;
            self.effect_presence.variables.insert(name.into(), Tracked { deps, #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] absent: self.effect_presence.absent });
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — only a necessary absence guard can authorize reconnect cancellation; payload dependencies cannot.
    pub(super) fn emission_presence(&mut self) -> Result<Dependencies, RuntimeError> {
        self.meter.heap(bytes(&self.effect_presence.guard))?;
        Ok(self.effect_presence.guard.clone())
    }

    pub(super) fn enter_presence_guard(&mut self, site: &str, take_then: bool) -> Result<Dependencies, RuntimeError> {
        let proof = self.effect_presence.guards.get(site);
        let (yes, no) = proof.map(|p| (p.yes, p.no)).unwrap_or_default();
        // Reserve the same guard copy on either arm. Only the proven arm
        // carries deadlines; None still reserves the potential allocation.
        self.meter.heap(bytes(&self.effect_presence.guard) + bytes(&self.effect_presence.reads))?;
        let mut deps = self.effect_presence.guard.clone();
        for (principal, deadline) in &self.effect_presence.reads {
            let deadline = if if take_then { yes } else { no } { *deadline } else { None };
            let entry = deps.entry(principal.clone()).or_default();
            *entry = (*entry).max(deadline);
        }
        Ok(std::mem::replace(&mut self.effect_presence.guard, deps))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#21.3 — project only proven absence guards; the ordinary scalar remains the payload's value.
    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    pub(super) fn presence_arm(&self, site: &str, actual: bool) -> bool {
        if self.device { return actual; }
        let Some(Scalar::Bool(absent)) = self.effect_presence.absent else { return actual; };
        let Some(proof) = self.effect_presence.guards.get(site) else { return actual; };
        if proof.setup || (!proof.is_setup && if absent { proof.yes && proof.emit_yes } else { proof.no && proof.emit_no }) { absent } else { actual }
    }

    #[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]
    pub(super) fn presence_arm(&self, _site: &str, actual: bool) -> bool { actual }

    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
    pub(super) fn presence_binary(&mut self, op: crate::ir::BinaryOp, left: &Value, projected: Option<Scalar>, right: &Value) {
        let other = self.effect_presence.absent.take();
        if projected.is_none() && other.is_none() { return; }
        if let (Some(l), Some(r)) = (projected.or_else(|| Scalar::of(left)), other.or_else(|| Scalar::of(right))) {
            self.effect_presence.absent = super::runner::binary(op, l.value(), r.value()).ok().as_ref().and_then(Scalar::of);
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn push_presence_value(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let deps = self.value_presence()?;
        if !deps.is_empty() {
            self.meter.heap(name.len() as u64 + 64 + bytes(&deps))?;
            let value = self.effect_presence.variables.entry(name.into()).or_insert_with(|| Tracked { deps: Dependencies::new(), #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] absent: None });
            #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
            { value.absent = None; }
            value.deps.extend(deps);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

// A proof is deliberately narrower than a dependency: for each boolean
// outcome, *every* presence input must be absent for that outcome to hold.
// False conjunctions and true disjunctions provide no such proof. Unknown
// forms retain ordinary emission semantics; no condition is re-executed.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
struct Proof { independent: bool, count: bool, yes: bool, no: bool }
impl Proof {
    const INDEPENDENT: Self = Self { independent: true, count: false, yes: true, no: true };
    const UNKNOWN: Self = Self { independent: false, count: false, yes: false, no: false };
    fn boolean(yes: bool, no: bool) -> Self { Self { yes, no, ..Self::UNKNOWN } }
    fn not(self) -> Self { Self { yes: self.no, no: self.yes, count: false, ..self } }
    fn combine(self, right: Self, and: bool) -> Self {
        if self.independent && right.independent { return Self::INDEPENDENT; }
        Self::boolean(and && self.yes && right.yes, !and && self.no && right.no)
    }
}

type Proofs<'a> = BTreeMap<&'a str, Proof>;

fn proof(expr: &Expr, env: &Proofs<'_>) -> Proof {
    use crate::ir::{BinaryOp as B, Builtin, UnaryOp};
    match expr {
        Expr::Variable(name) => env.get(name.as_str()).copied().unwrap_or(Proof::INDEPENDENT),
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Input(_) => Proof::INDEPENDENT,
        Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Not, value } => proof(value, env).not(),
        Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::Connected, args } if args.iter().all(|e| proof(e, env).independent) => Proof { count: true, ..Proof::UNKNOWN },
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right } => {
            let l = proof(left, env);
            let r = proof(right, env);
            if *op == B::And || *op == B::Or { return l.combine(r, *op == B::And); }
            if l.independent && r.independent { return Proof::INDEPENDENT; }
            if let Expr::Literal(Value::Bool(value)) = **right {
                if *op == B::Eq { return if value { l } else { l.not() }; }
                if *op == B::Ne { return if value { l.not() } else { l }; }
            }
            if l.count && matches!(**right, Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0))) {
                return match op { B::Eq | B::Lte => Proof::boolean(true, false), B::Ne | B::Gt => Proof::boolean(false, true), _ => Proof::UNKNOWN };
            }
            if r.count && matches!(**left, Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0))) {
                return match op { B::Eq | B::Gte => Proof::boolean(true, false), B::Ne | B::Lt => Proof::boolean(false, true), _ => Proof::UNKNOWN };
            }
            Proof::UNKNOWN
        }
        Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } if proof(base, env).independent => Proof::INDEPENDENT,
        Expr::Builtin { args, .. } if args.iter().all(|e| proof(e, env).independent) => Proof::INDEPENDENT,
        _ => Proof::UNKNOWN,
    }
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
fn emits(body: &[Stmt]) -> bool {
    body.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt {
        Stmt::Emit { .. } => true,
        Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => emits(then_body) || emits(else_body),
        Stmt::For { body, .. } => emits(body),
        _ => false,
    })
}

fn assigns(body: &[crate::ir::Stmt], name: &str) -> bool {
    use crate::ir::Stmt;
    body.iter().any(|s| match s {
        Stmt::Let { name: n, .. } | Stmt::Assign { name: n, .. } | Stmt::Const { name: n, .. } | Stmt::Push { target: n, .. } => n == name,
        Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => assigns(then_body, name) || assigns(else_body, name),
        Stmt::For { body, .. } => assigns(body, name),
        _ => false,
    })
}

fn copy_proofs<'a>(env: &Proofs<'a>, meter: &mut crate::meter::Meter) -> Result<Proofs<'a>, RuntimeError> {
    meter.heap(env.len() as u64 * 64)?;
    Ok(env.clone())
}

fn plan_guards<'a>(body: &'a [crate::ir::Stmt], prefix: &str, env: &mut Proofs<'a>, guards: &mut BTreeMap<String, Guard>, meter: &mut crate::meter::Meter, #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] setups: &mut Setups) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    use crate::ir::Stmt;
    for (index, stmt) in body.iter().enumerate() {
        meter.heap(prefix.len() as u64 + 32)?;
        let site = format!("{prefix}:{}", index + 1);
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Let { name, value } | Stmt::Assign { name, value } | Stmt::Const { name, value } => {
                let p = proof(value, env);
                #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
                if p.independent { setups.independent.insert(stmt as *const Stmt as usize); }
                meter.heap(64)?;
                env.insert(name, p);
            }
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                let p = proof(condition, env);
                #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
                setups.guards.insert(stmt as *const Stmt as usize, site.clone());
                meter.heap(site.len() as u64 + 64)?;
                guards.insert(site.clone(), Guard {
                    yes: p.yes, no: p.no,
                    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
                    setup: false,
                    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
                    is_setup: short_target(condition, then_body, else_body).is_some(),
                    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
                    emit_yes: emits(then_body),
                    #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
                    emit_no: emits(else_body),
                });
                let mut yes = copy_proofs(env, meter)?;
                let mut no = copy_proofs(env, meter)?;
                meter.heap(2 * site.len() as u64 + 10)?;
                plan_guards(then_body, &format!("{site}:then"), &mut yes, guards, meter, #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] setups)?;
                plan_guards(else_body, &format!("{site}:else"), &mut no, guards, meter, #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] setups)?;
                // The lowered short circuit is `let b = left; if b:
                // b = right` (and), or its else arm (or). Reconstruct its
                // boolean proof using both alternatives, even when one skips.
                let short = if let Expr::Variable(name) = condition {
                    let (branch, right, and) = if else_body.is_empty() { (then_body, &yes, true) } else { (else_body, &no, false) };
                    let other_empty = if and { else_body.is_empty() } else { then_body.is_empty() };
                    if other_empty && matches!(branch.last(), Some(Stmt::Assign { name: target, .. }) if target == name) {
                        right.get(name.as_str()).map(|r| (name.as_str(), p.combine(*r, and)))
                    } else { None }
                } else { None };
                for (&name, value) in yes.iter().chain(no.iter()) {
                    if assigns(then_body, name) || assigns(else_body, name) {
                        let merged = if let Some((_, result)) = short.filter(|(target, _)| *target == name) { result }
                            else if p.independent && value.independent && yes.get(name).is_some_and(|v| v.independent) && no.get(name).is_some_and(|v| v.independent) { Proof::INDEPENDENT }
                            else { Proof::UNKNOWN };
                        meter.heap(64)?;
                        env.insert(name, merged);
                    }
                }
            }
            Stmt::For { binding, body, .. } => {
                // Loop-carried values are not boolean proofs; a literal/direct
                // absence guard inside the loop still qualifies on every row.
                for (name, value) in env.iter_mut() { if assigns(body, name) { *value = Proof::UNKNOWN; } }
                meter.heap(64)?;
                env.insert(binding, Proof::INDEPENDENT);
                plan_guards(body, &site, env, guards, meter, #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))] setups)?;
                for (name, value) in env.iter_mut() { if assigns(body, name) { *value = Proof::UNKNOWN; } }
            }
            Stmt::Push { target, .. } => { meter.heap(64)?; env.insert(target, Proof::UNKNOWN); }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

// Quarry lowers a boolean expression to a private accumulator and one
// assignment arm. Project that setup only when its result feeds a proven
// guard, never for payloads or an unproven enclosing boolean expression.
#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
pub(super) fn short_target<'a>(condition: &'a Expr, then_body: &[Stmt], else_body: &[Stmt]) -> Option<&'a str> {
    let Expr::Variable(name) = condition else { return None; };
    if !name.starts_with("$bool") { return None; }
    let branch = if else_body.is_empty() { then_body } else if then_body.is_empty() { else_body } else { return None; };
    matches!(branch.last(), Some(Stmt::Assign { name: target, .. }) if target == name).then_some(name)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — scoped setup invalidation follows uses before undo, preserving definition marks and every meter charge.
#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
#[derive(Default)]
struct Setups {
    independent: HashSet<usize>,
    guards: HashMap<usize, String>,
    inputs: HashMap<usize, Vec<usize>>,
    roots: Vec<usize>,
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct Binding { statement: usize, loop_depth: usize, loop_scope: usize }

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
#[derive(Default)]
struct Bindings<'a> {
    values: HashMap<&'a str, Binding>,
    undo: Vec<(&'a str, Option<Binding>)>,
    loops: Vec<usize>,
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
impl<'a> Bindings<'a> {
    fn get(&self, name: &str, assignments: &Assignments<'_>) -> Option<&usize> {
        let value = self.values.get(name)?;
        if value.loop_depth > 0 && self.loops.get(value.loop_depth - 1) != Some(&value.loop_scope) { return None; }
        // Only the outermost loop entered since this definition matters:
        // its assignment range contains every nested loop's range as well.
        if self.loops.get(value.loop_depth).is_some_and(|scope| assignments.assigns(*scope, name)) { return None; }
        Some(&value.statement)
    }
    fn set(&mut self, name: &'a str, value: Option<usize>) {
        let value = value.map(|statement| Binding { statement, loop_depth: self.loops.len(), loop_scope: self.loops.last().copied().unwrap_or(0) });
        let old = self.values.get(name).copied();
        if old == value { return; }
        self.undo.push((name, old));
        if let Some(value) = value { self.values.insert(name, value); } else { self.values.remove(name); }
    }
    fn restore(&mut self, mark: usize) {
        while self.undo.len() > mark {
            let (name, old) = self.undo.pop().unwrap();
            if let Some(old) = old { self.values.insert(name, old); } else { self.values.remove(name); }
        }
    }
}

// A join only needs to forget names read before this scope is undone. The
// enclosing join owns reads outside it. Build those scoped kills backwards,
// merging smaller name sets into larger ones: descendant assignments are
// never enumerated again at every ancestor. Short-circuit arms include their
// implicit result read. Loop entry is lazy: a name's sorted assignment sites
// answer membership in the outermost newly entered loop with one search.
#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
#[derive(Default)]
struct Assignments<'a> {
    exits: HashMap<usize, Vec<&'a str>>,
    positions: HashMap<&'a str, Vec<usize>>,
    loops: HashMap<usize, std::ops::Range<usize>>,
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
impl<'a> Assignments<'a> {
    fn index(&mut self, body: &'a [Stmt], next: &mut usize) {
        for stmt in body {
            match stmt {
                Stmt::Let { name, .. } | Stmt::Assign { name, .. } | Stmt::Const { name, .. } | Stmt::Push { target: name, .. } => {
                    self.positions.entry(name).or_default().push(*next);
                    *next += 1;
                }
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { self.index(then_body, next); self.index(else_body, next); }
                Stmt::For { body, .. } => {
                    let start = *next;
                    self.index(body, next);
                    self.loops.insert(stmt as *const Stmt as usize, start..*next);
                }
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn assigns(&self, scope: usize, name: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(positions) = self.positions.get(name) else { return false; };
        let range = &self.loops[&scope];
        positions.get(positions.partition_point(|p| *p < range.start)).is_some_and(|p| *p < range.end)
    }
    fn merge(into: &mut HashSet<&'a str>, mut other: HashSet<&'a str>) {
        if into.len() < other.len() { std::mem::swap(into, &mut other); }
        into.extend(other);
    }
    fn intersection(a: &HashSet<&'a str>, b: &HashSet<&'a str>) -> Vec<&'a str> {
        let (small, large) = if a.len() < b.len() { (a, b) } else { (b, a) };
        small.iter().copied().filter(|name| large.contains(name)).collect()
    }
    fn reads(expr: &'a Expr, names: &mut HashSet<&'a str>) {
        match expr {
            Expr::Variable(name) => { names.insert(name); }
            Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => Self::reads(base, names),
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { Self::reads(left, names); Self::reads(right, names); }
            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => { for arg in args { Self::reads(arg, names); } }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    fn collect(&mut self, body: &'a [Stmt], result: Option<&'a str>) -> (HashSet<&'a str>, HashSet<&'a str>) {
        let mut written = HashSet::new();
        let mut read = HashSet::new();
        read.extend(result);
        for stmt in body.iter().rev() {
            let statement = stmt as *const Stmt as usize;
            match stmt {
                Stmt::Let { name, value } | Stmt::Assign { name, value } | Stmt::Const { name, value } => {
                    written.insert(name.as_str());
                    Self::reads(value, &mut read);
                }
                Stmt::Push { target, .. } => { written.insert(target.as_str()); }
                Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                    let target = short_target(condition, then_body, else_body);
                    let (mut writes, mut reads) = self.collect(then_body, target.filter(|_| else_body.is_empty()));
                    let (other_writes, other_reads) = self.collect(else_body, target.filter(|_| !else_body.is_empty()));
                    Self::merge(&mut writes, other_writes);
                    Self::merge(&mut reads, other_reads);
                    self.exits.insert(statement, Self::intersection(&writes, &read));
                    Self::merge(&mut written, writes);
                    Self::merge(&mut read, reads);
                    Self::reads(condition, &mut read);
                }
                Stmt::For { body, .. } => {
                    let (writes, reads) = self.collect(body, None);
                    self.exits.insert(statement, Self::intersection(&writes, &read));
                    Self::merge(&mut written, writes);
                    Self::merge(&mut read, reads);
                }
                _ => {}
            }
        }
        (written, read)
    }
    fn forget(&self, statement: usize, bindings: &mut Bindings<'a>) {
        for &name in &self.exits[&statement] { bindings.set(name, None); }
    }
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
fn mark_inputs(expr: &Expr, bindings: &Bindings<'_>, inputs: &mut Vec<usize>, assignments: &Assignments<'_>) {
    match expr {
        Expr::Variable(name) => { if let Some(binding) = bindings.get(name.as_str(), assignments) { inputs.push(*binding); } }
        Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => mark_inputs(base, bindings, inputs, assignments),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { mark_inputs(left, bindings, inputs, assignments); mark_inputs(right, bindings, inputs, assignments); }
        Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => { for arg in args { mark_inputs(arg, bindings, inputs, assignments); } }
        _ => {}
    }
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
fn mark_bindings(setups: &Setups, guards: &mut BTreeMap<String, Guard>) {
    let mut visited = HashSet::new();
    let mut pending = setups.roots.clone();
    while let Some(statement) = pending.pop() {
        if !visited.insert(statement) { continue; }
        if let Some(site) = setups.guards.get(&statement) { guards.get_mut(site).unwrap().setup = true; }
        if let Some(inputs) = setups.inputs.get(&statement) { pending.extend(inputs); }
    }
}

// Resolve each input to the reaching statement, not its spelling: assigning
// the same variable later must not reuse an earlier definition's visited bit.
// Independent definitions are absent from the graph. No backwards name scan
// or recursive alias expansion is needed, even for a diamond-shaped DAG.
#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
fn needed_setups<'a>(body: &'a [Stmt], bindings: &mut Bindings<'a>, guards: &BTreeMap<String, Guard>, setups: &mut Setups) {
    let mut assignments = Assignments::default();
    assignments.index(body, &mut 0);
    assignments.collect(body, None);
    discover_setups(body, bindings, guards, setups, &assignments);
}

#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
fn discover_setups<'a>(body: &'a [Stmt], bindings: &mut Bindings<'a>, guards: &BTreeMap<String, Guard>, setups: &mut Setups, assignments: &Assignments<'a>) {
    for stmt in body {
        let statement = stmt as *const Stmt as usize;
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Let { name, value } | Stmt::Assign { name, value } | Stmt::Const { name, value } => {
                if setups.independent.contains(&statement) { bindings.set(name, None); }
                else {
                    let mut inputs = Vec::new();
                    mark_inputs(value, bindings, &mut inputs, assignments);
                    setups.inputs.insert(statement, inputs);
                    bindings.set(name, Some(statement));
                }
            }
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                let guard = &guards[&setups.guards[&statement]];
                if !guard.is_setup && ((guard.yes && guard.emit_yes) || (guard.no && guard.emit_no)) {
                    mark_inputs(condition, bindings, &mut setups.roots, assignments);
                }
                let target = short_target(condition, then_body, else_body);
                let mut inputs = Vec::new();
                if target.is_some() { mark_inputs(condition, bindings, &mut inputs, assignments); }
                let mark = bindings.undo.len();
                discover_setups(then_body, bindings, guards, setups, assignments);
                if else_body.is_empty() {
                    if let Some(binding) = target.and_then(|name| bindings.get(name, assignments)) { inputs.push(*binding); }
                }
                bindings.restore(mark);
                discover_setups(else_body, bindings, guards, setups, assignments);
                if !else_body.is_empty() {
                    if let Some(binding) = target.and_then(|name| bindings.get(name, assignments)) { inputs.push(*binding); }
                }
                bindings.restore(mark);
                assignments.forget(statement, bindings);
                if let Some(name) = target {
                    if inputs.is_empty() { bindings.set(name, None); }
                    else { setups.inputs.insert(statement, inputs); bindings.set(name, Some(statement)); }
                }
            }
            Stmt::For { binding, body, .. } => {
                bindings.loops.push(statement);
                bindings.set(binding, None);
                discover_setups(body, bindings, guards, setups, assignments);
                bindings.loops.pop();
                assignments.forget(statement, bindings);
            }
            Stmt::Push { target, .. } => bindings.set(target, None),
            _ => {}
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(all(test, not(target_arch = "wasm32")))]
#[path = "../../tests/presence/setups.rs"]
mod setup_tests;
//! Flow of server presence through the bound mutation, including loop back edges.
use snapback4_core::ir::{self, Expr, LoopDomain, Program, Stmt};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Execution, ResidencySite, SiteClass};
use snapback4_core::engine::prediction::{total_body, total_expr};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

use super::super::program::setup::{Owner, Ownership, runtime_path};

type Origins = BTreeSet<(String, String)>;
type Env = BTreeMap<String, Origins>;
const REWRITE: &str = "decide only the emit with presence; read it on the server in a service mutation for anything else";

// @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — only presence flows ending in effects preserve prediction; every other sink stays server-only.
pub(super) fn inspect(program: &Program, prediction_body: &[Stmt], fact: &mut Execution, ownership: &Ownership, sources: &[(Expr, crate::diag::Site, String)]) {
    let mut flow = Flow { seen: super::effect_reads::uses_presence(program), sites: BTreeMap::new(), consumers: BTreeMap::new(), effect_control: Origins::new(), escaped: Env::new(), ownership, sources, program: &program.name, guards: BTreeSet::new() };
    flow.body(prediction_body, &mut Env::new(), &Origins::new(), "", false);
    fact.connected_effects_only = flow.seen && flow.sites.is_empty();
    if fact.connected_effects_only { fact.prediction_guards = flow.guards.into_iter().collect(); }
    for (key, site) in flow.sites {
        if let Some(consumers) = flow.consumers.get(&key) {
            for consumer in consumers {
                let mut located = site.clone(); located.site = consumer.clone();
                fact.sites.push(located);
            }
        } else { fact.sites.push(site); }
    }
}

struct Flow<'a> {
    seen: bool,
    sites: BTreeMap<(String, String, String), ResidencySite>,
    consumers: BTreeMap<(String, String, String), BTreeSet<String>>,
    effect_control: Origins,
    escaped: Env,
    ownership: &'a Ownership,
    sources: &'a [(Expr, crate::diag::Site, String)],
    program: &'a str,
    guards: BTreeSet<String>,
}

impl Flow<'_> {
    fn reject(&mut self, origins: &Origins, usage: &str, site: &str) {
        for (origin, location) in origins {
            self.sites.entry((site.into(), usage.into(), format!("{origin} {location}"))).or_insert_with(|| ResidencySite {
                // @ref LLP 3000.011.000.000#6 — the existing read site locates the consumer; the reason retains its presence origin.
                site: if site.starts_with("programs.") { site.into() } else { location.clone() }, table: "connected".into(), index: "principal".into(), kind: "presence".into(), class: SiteClass::Server,
                reason: format!("{origin} decides {usage} (read at {location})"), rewrite: REWRITE.into(),
            });
        }
    }

    fn sink(&mut self, value: &Expr, env: &mut Env, control: &Origins, usage: &str, site: &str) -> Origins {
        let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, site);
        origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
        if usage.starts_with("a write") || usage.starts_with("a require") || usage == "a check/refusal" { origins.extend(self.effect_control.iter().cloned()); }
        self.reject(&origins, usage, site);
        origins
    }

    // Assignments replace a slot on this path; branches join their slots. A
    // loop joins its entry and back edge until stable, so a later assignment
    // cannot hide a presence use in the next iteration. Binder slots are unique.
    fn body(&mut self, body: &[Stmt], env: &mut Env, control: &Origins, path: &str, block_value: bool) -> Origins {
        let mut returned = Origins::new();
        for (n, stmt) in body.iter().enumerate() {
            let site = if path.is_empty() { n.to_string() } else { format!("{path}.{n}") };
            let owner = self.ownership.get(&site).map(|owner| owner.kind);
            let block_value = block_value || owner.is_some();
            let before_effect = self.effect_control.clone();
            let empty = Origins::new();
            let control = if owner == Some(Owner::Payload) {
                self.effect_control.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                &empty
            } else { control };
            match stmt {
                Stmt::Const { name, value } | Stmt::Let { name, value } | Stmt::Assign { name, value } => {
                    let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, &site);
                    // A presence-selected variable-width value could make later
                    // clones cost more on the server even after both arms paid.
                    if block_value && !fixed_scalar(value) { self.reject(control, "a variable-size conditional value", &site); }
                    origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    env.insert(name.clone(), origins);
                }
                Stmt::Push { target, value } => {
                    let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, &site);
                    origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    env.entry(target.clone()).or_default().extend(origins);
                }
                Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                    let mut branch = self.expr(condition, env, control, &site);
                    if !branch.is_empty() {
                        if !total_body(then_body) || !total_body(else_body) { self.reject(&branch, "a fallible or variable-cost arm", &site); }
                        let runtime = runtime_path(self.program, &site);
                        if !runtime.is_empty() { self.guards.insert(runtime); }
                        else { self.reject(&branch, "an arm without a prediction charge plan", &site); }
                    }
                    branch.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    // Owned short-circuit/coalesce setup computes values;
                    // the remaining source statement arms must
                    // contain only emits. Sinks inside either form still reject below.
                    if !block_value && let Some(usage) = non_effect(then_body, &format!("{site}.then"), self.ownership).or_else(|| non_effect(else_body, &format!("{site}.else"), self.ownership)) { self.reject(&branch, usage, &site); }
                    let mut then_env = env.clone();
                    returned.extend(self.body(then_body, &mut then_env, &branch, &format!("{site}.then"), block_value));
                    let mut else_env = env.clone();
                    returned.extend(self.body(else_body, &mut else_env, &branch, &format!("{site}.else"), block_value));
                    *env = join(then_env, &else_env);
                }
                Stmt::For { binding, domain, body } => {
                    let origins = match domain {
                        LoopDomain::Page(scan) => self.scan(scan, env, control),
                        LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => {
                            let mut origins = self.expr(start, env, control, &site);
                            origins.extend(self.expr(end_exclusive, env, control, &site)); origins
                        }
                        LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => self.expr(value, env, control, &site),
                        LoopDomain::ArgList { name, .. } => env.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_default(),
                        LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => self.sink(&recurse.seed, env, control, "a read key", &recurse.site),
                        LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } => Origins::new(),
                    };
                    let mut branch = control.clone(); branch.extend(origins);
                    self.reject(&branch, "a loop domain", &site);
                    loop {
                        let before = env.clone();
                        let mut iteration = before.clone();
                        iteration.insert(binding.clone(), branch.clone());
                        returned.extend(self.body(body, &mut iteration, &branch, &format!("{site}.loop"), block_value));
                        *env = join(before.clone(), &iteration);
                        if *env == before { break; }
                    }
                }
                Stmt::Require { condition, code, site: location, .. } => {
                    self.sink(condition, env, control, &format!("a require {code}"), location.as_deref().unwrap_or(&site));
                }
                Stmt::Check { condition, current } => {
                    self.sink(condition, env, control, "a check/refusal", &site);
                    self.read(current, env, control, "a read key");
                }
                Stmt::Insert { table, row } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table, row, .. } => {
                    let origins = self.sink(row, env, control, "a write row", &format!("{site} insert {table}"));
                    if let Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { result, .. } = stmt { env.insert(result.clone(), origins); }
                }
                Stmt::Update { table, key, patch } | Stmt::Upsert { table, key, row: patch } => {
                    self.sink(key, env, control, "a write key", &format!("{site} write {table}"));
                    self.sink(patch, env, control, "a write patch", &format!("{site} write {table}"));
                }
                Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, condition, result, .. } => {
                    let mut origins = self.read(target, env, control, "a write key");
                    origins.extend(self.sink(patch, env, control, "a write patch", &target.site));
                    if let Some(condition) = condition {
                        let mut local = env.clone();
                        local.insert(condition.old.clone(), origins.clone());
                        origins.extend(self.body(&condition.setup, &mut local, control, &format!("{site}.condition"), true));
                        origins.extend(self.sink(&condition.value, &mut local, control, "a write condition", &target.site));
                    }
                    if let Some(result) = result { env.insert(result.clone(), origins); }
                }
                Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => for key in &target.key { self.sink(key, env, control, "a write key", &format!("{site} delete {}", target.table)); },
                Stmt::Delete { table, key } => { self.sink(key, env, control, "a write key", &format!("{site} delete {table}")); }
                Stmt::Return(value) => {
                    let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, &site);
                    origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    if !block_value { self.reject(&origins, "the returned value", &site); }
                    returned.extend(origins);
                }
                Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                    // A guard on emit need not taint a constant read key inside its
                    // payload. It still controls any write/refusal a helper performs.
                    let before = self.effect_control.clone();
                    self.effect_control.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    self.expr(payload, env, &Origins::new(), &site);
                    if let Some(key) = once { self.expr(key, env, &Origins::new(), &site); }
                    self.effect_control = before;
                }
                Stmt::Break => self.reject(control, "break", &site),
                Stmt::Continue => self.reject(control, "continue", &site),
            }
            self.effect_control = before_effect;
            // An exit skips later overwrites. Keep every possible exit image
            // conservatively, including a continue's back edge and a break's
            // loop exit; joining only the ordinary branch tail loses these.
            if matches!(stmt, Stmt::Return(_) | Stmt::Break | Stmt::Continue) {
                self.escaped = join(std::mem::take(&mut self.escaped), env);
                break;
            }
            *env = join(std::mem::take(env), &self.escaped);
        }
        returned
    }

    fn expr(&mut self, value: &Expr, env: &mut Env, control: &Origins, site: &str) -> Origins {
        if !total_expr(value) {
            let mut controlled = control.clone();
            controlled.extend(self.effect_control.iter().cloned());
            self.reject(&controlled, "a fallible evaluation", site);
            // @ref LLP 3000.011.000.000#6 — attach already-bound consumers to the existing cause, without changing its analysis or deduplication key.
            let consumer = match value {
                Expr::Get(read) | Expr::Exists(read) => Some(&read.site),
                Expr::Scan(scan) => Some(&scan.site),
                Expr::Merge(merge) => Some(&merge.site),
                _ => None,
            };
            if let Some(consumer) = consumer {
                for (origin, location) in &controlled {
                    self.consumers.entry((site.into(), "a fallible evaluation".into(), format!("{origin} {location}"))).or_default().insert(consumer.clone());
                }
            }
        }
        match value {
            Expr::Variable(name) => env.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_default(),
            Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Input(_) => Origins::new(),
            Expr::Builtin { builtin, args } => {
                let mut origins = Origins::new();
                for arg in args { origins.extend(self.expr(arg, env, control, site)); }
                if *builtin == ir::Builtin::Connected {
                    self.seen = true;
                    self.reject(&origins, "a presence read key", site);
                    let matches: Vec<_> = self.sources.iter().filter(|(key, _, _)| args.first() == Some(key)).collect();
                    if matches.is_empty() {
                        origins.insert((format!("connected[{}]", args.first().map(label).unwrap_or_else(|| "principal".into())), "connected".into()));
                    } else {
                        // Identical lowered reads can occur at several source locations;
                        // retain all of them instead of inventing one deciding location.
                        let locations = matches.iter().map(|(_, site, _)| site.to_string()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                        let names = matches.iter().map(|(_, _, name)| name.clone()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" / ");
                        origins.insert((names, locations));
                    }
                }
                else {
                    let usage = match builtin { ir::Builtin::Divmod { .. } | ir::Builtin::Divide { .. } => "fallible division", ir::Builtin::Slice => "a variable-size slice", _ => "a fallible or variable-cost builtin" };
                    self.reject(&origins, usage, site);
                }
                origins
            }
            Expr::Field { base, .. } => self.expr(base, env, control, site),
            Expr::Unary { op, value: base } => {
                let origins = self.expr(base, env, control, site);
                if *op == ir::UnaryOp::Neg { self.reject(&origins, "fallible negation", site); }
                origins
            }
            Expr::Array(values) => {
                let mut origins = Origins::new();
                for value in values { origins.extend(self.expr(value, env, control, site)); } origins
            }
            Expr::Object(fields) => {
                let mut origins = Origins::new();
                for value in fields.values() { origins.extend(self.expr(value, env, control, site)); } origins
            }
            Expr::Shape { base, fields } => {
                let mut origins = self.expr(base, env, control, site);
                for value in fields.values() { origins.extend(self.expr(value, env, control, site)); } origins
            }
            Expr::Binary { op, left, right } => {
                let mut origins = self.expr(left, env, control, site);
                if matches!(op, ir::BinaryOp::And | ir::BinaryOp::Or) { self.reject(&origins, "short-circuit evaluation without a prediction charge plan", site); }
                origins.extend(self.expr(right, env, control, site));
                if matches!(op, ir::BinaryOp::Add | ir::BinaryOp::Sub | ir::BinaryOp::Mul) { self.reject(&origins, "fallible arithmetic", site); }
                origins
            }
            Expr::Get(read) | Expr::Exists(read) => self.read(read, env, control, "a read key"),
            Expr::Block { body, value } => {
                let mut local = env.clone();
                let mut origins = self.body(body, &mut local, control, &format!("{site}.value"), true);
                origins.extend(self.expr(value, &mut local, control, site));
                // Blocks can update a caller slot; join conservatively across early returns.
                for (name, before) in env.iter_mut() { if let Some(after) = local.get(name) { before.extend(after.iter().cloned()); } }
                origins
            }
            Expr::Scan(scan) => self.scan(scan, env, control),
            Expr::Merge(merge) => {
                let mut origins = Origins::new();
                for lane in &merge.lanes {
                    let mut local = env.clone();
                    if let Some(source) = &lane.source {
                        let mut origins_of_source = source.scan().map_or_else(Origins::new, |scan| self.scan(scan, &mut local, control));
                        for value in source.expressions() { origins_of_source.extend(self.expr(value, &mut local, control, &merge.site)); }
                        local.insert(lane.binding.clone(), origins_of_source.clone()); origins.extend(origins_of_source);
                    }
                    origins.extend(self.body(&lane.setup, &mut local, control, &format!("{}.lane", merge.site), true));
                    origins.extend(self.scan(&lane.scan, &mut local, control));
                }
                origins
            }
        }
    }

    fn read(&mut self, read: &ir::Read, env: &mut Env, control: &Origins, usage: &str) -> Origins {
        let mut origins = Origins::new();
        for key in &read.key { origins.extend(self.sink(key, env, control, usage, &read.site)); }
        origins
    }

    fn scan(&mut self, scan: &ir::Scan, env: &mut Env, control: &Origins) -> Origins {
        let mut origins = Origins::new();
        for key in &scan.prefix {
            let (ir::IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(value) | ir::IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(value)) = key;
            origins.extend(self.sink(value, env, control, "a read key", &scan.site));
        }
        for value in [&scan.after, &scan.before].into_iter().flatten() { origins.extend(self.sink(value, env, control, "a read cursor", &scan.site)); }
        if let Some(range) = &scan.range {
            for value in [&range.gt, &range.gte, &range.lt, &range.lte].into_iter().flatten() { origins.extend(self.sink(value, env, control, "a read key", &scan.site)); }
        }
        if let Some(filter) = &scan.filter { origins.extend(self.sink(filter, env, control, "a read condition", &scan.site)); }
        if let ir::Bound::Arg { name, .. } = &scan.take {
            let bound = env.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_default();
            self.reject(&bound, "a read bound", &scan.site); origins.extend(bound);
        }
        origins
    }
}

fn fixed_scalar(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    match value {
        Expr::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Int(_) | snapback4_core::Value::Bool(_) | snapback4_core::Value::Null) => true,
        Expr::Builtin { builtin: ir::Builtin::Connected, .. } | Expr::Unary { op: ir::UnaryOp::Not, .. } => true,
        Expr::Binary { op, .. } => matches!(op, ir::BinaryOp::Eq | ir::BinaryOp::Ne | ir::BinaryOp::Lt | ir::BinaryOp::Lte | ir::BinaryOp::Gt | ir::BinaryOp::Gte | ir::BinaryOp::And | ir::BinaryOp::Or),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn join(mut a: Env, b: &Env) -> Env {
    for (name, origins) in b { a.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(origins.iter().cloned()); }
    a
}

fn non_effect(body: &[Stmt], path: &str, ownership: &Ownership) -> Option<&'static str> {
    let mut assignment = None;
    for (index, stmt) in body.iter().enumerate() {
        let site = format!("{path}.{index}");
        if ownership.contains_key(&site) { continue; }
        let usage = match stmt {
            Stmt::Emit { .. } => None,
            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => non_effect(then_body, &format!("{site}.then"), ownership).or_else(|| non_effect(else_body, &format!("{site}.else"), ownership)),
            Stmt::Insert { .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { .. } | Stmt::Update { .. } | Stmt::Upsert { .. } | Stmt::WriteTarget { .. } | Stmt::Delete { .. } | Stmt::DeleteTarget { .. } => Some("a write"),
            Stmt::Break => Some("break"),
            Stmt::Continue => Some("continue"),
            Stmt::Return(_) => Some("the returned value"),
            Stmt::Require { .. } | Stmt::Check { .. } => Some("a require/check refusal"),
            Stmt::For { .. } => Some("a loop"),
            Stmt::Const { .. } | Stmt::Let { .. } | Stmt::Assign { .. } | Stmt::Push { .. } => { assignment = Some("an assignment"); None }
        };
        if usage.is_some() { return usage; }
    }
    assignment
}

fn label(value: &Expr) -> String {
    match value {
        Expr::Variable(name) => if let Some(name) = name.strip_prefix('$') { name.trim_end_matches(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit()).into() } else { name.clone() },
        Expr::Input(ir::Input::Viewer) => "viewer".into(),
        Expr::Field { base, name } => format!("{}.{name}", label(base)),
        _ => "principal expression".into(),
    }
}

/// A connection lasts as long as its poll and a grace after it: a client
/// re-polls within milliseconds of an answer, a dropped one never does.
use snapback4_core::engine::{PRESENCE_GRACE, PRESENCE_CONFIRMATION, PRESENCE_REFRESH};

/// The registry of open long polls, per principal: how many are open now,
/// and when the last one ended. A device that holds `/changes?wait` open
/// (every tier does) is connected; nothing else is.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Live {
    polls: Mutex<BTreeMap<String, (u32, Option<i64>)>>,
    clock: Arc<Mutex<crate::dev_clock::Clock>>,
}

impl Live {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — presence remains server knowledge; development grace uses the same clock as the commit deciding effects.
    fn new(clock: Arc<Mutex<crate::dev_clock::Clock>>) -> Self { Self { polls: Mutex::new(BTreeMap::new()), clock } }

    fn enter(&self, principal: &str) {
        let mut map = lock(&self.polls);
        map.entry(principal.to_owned()).or_insert((0, None)).0 += 1;
    }

    fn leave(&self, principal: &str) {
        let mut map = lock(&self.polls);
        let now = lock(&self.clock).presence_now();
        if let Some(entry) = map.get_mut(principal) {
            entry.0 = entry.0.saturating_sub(1);
            entry.1 = Some(now);
        }
        map.retain(|_, (open, last)| *open > 0 || last.is_some_and(|at| now.saturating_sub(at) < PRESENCE_GRACE.as_millis() as i64));
    }
}

impl Presence for Live {
    fn effect_presence(&self, principal: &str) -> (u64, Option<i64>) {
        let now = lock(&self.clock).now();
        self.effect_presence_at(principal, now)
    }

    fn effect_presence_at(&self, principal: &str, now: i64) -> (u64, Option<i64>) {
        let map = lock(&self.polls);
        let clock = lock(&self.clock);
        match map.get(principal) {
            Some((open, _)) if *open > 0 => (u64::from(*open), Some(now.saturating_add(PRESENCE_CONFIRMATION.as_millis() as i64))),
            Some((_, Some(last))) => {
                let remaining = (PRESENCE_GRACE.as_millis() as i64).saturating_sub(clock.presence_now().saturating_sub(*last));
                (0, (remaining > 0).then(|| clock.now().saturating_add(remaining)))
            }
            _ => (0, None),
        }
    }

    fn connections(&self, principal: &str) -> u64 {
        let map = lock(&self.polls);
        let now = lock(&self.clock).presence_now();
        match map.get(principal) {
            Some((open, _)) if *open > 0 => u64::from(*open),
            Some((_, Some(last))) if now.saturating_sub(*last) < PRESENCE_GRACE.as_millis() as i64 => 1,
            _ => 0,
        }
    }
}
